Stop Hunting for Pricey Antiques—Designers Say These 9 Humble Finds Give a Home So Much Character

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

They’re affordable, widely available, and stylish.

It’s any avid thrifter’s dream to stumble upon an extremely valuable piece of decor while browsing the shelves of their favorite antique store. But while you’re hunting for rare treasures, don’t discount the less-expensive finds—they can be the secret to a home that looks instantly filled with character.

“To me, a collected home is much more interesting when everything isn’t perfect or chosen for its monetary value,” designer Lauren Sullivan of Well by Design tells House Beautiful. “Often, it’s the less precious pieces that give a room its personality.”

Even as older generations are continuing to downsize and offload unwanted things to local thrift stores, it still might be hard to find the diamonds in the rough. Keep reading to find out what Sullivan, and a handful of other designers, say you should be on the lookout for on your next thrifting visit.

The most valuable antiques are the ones that have been passed down through generations. In Sullivan’s own bathroom, the designer keeps her great-grandmother’s vanity set on the countertop, its aged finish providing beautiful contrast with the more contemporary marble and finishes. In her bedroom, the same great-grandmother’s small bedside clock found a place on Sullivan’s nightstand.

“Neither is particularly valuable monetarily, but I love incorporating pieces that have been passed down through generations into a home filled with both old and new,” Sullivan says.

Designers love incorporating artwork into homes to add personality and dimension, but Sullivan, as well as designer Becca Meyer of BB Meyer Design, believe that antique shops are where you can really find some hidden gems.

“Some of my favorite pieces of art have come from flea markets or antique shops for very little money,” Meyer says. “I much prefer these to mass produced prints.” You’re more likely to find pieces with aged textures at these types of shops, and that patinaed quality creates an entirely unique look.

Sullivan explains that she’s actually particularly drawn to prints, as well as smaller paintings and silhouettes. She even has an inexpensive print of The Skating Minister in a vintage frame in one of her guest rooms, pictured above. “These smaller, or sometimes quirky, pieces can bring so much personality to a space without requiring a significant investment,” she adds. They may not be originals, but they’re definitely unique.

Old pottery and ceramics are widely available, and they are usually affordable as well. Designer Brooke Snyder of Design Hutch loves how they make any place feel more curated.

The imperfections these handmade pieces carry, from uneven shapes, variations in glaze, or evidence of years of use, are what help these items bring a warmth to a room, particularly when placed near more refined materials, according to Sullivan. They feel humble, maybe even utilitarian, especially when displayed in a kitchen. Or, better yet, used in a kitchen (as long as they’re safe).

“They're beautiful on their own but also so functional for holding utensils or branches or flowers,” Meyer adds. “They make any space feel a little more lived-in and loved and help tell a story—and they don't break the bank!”

“I love incorporating vintage plates, old copper pots, and woven baskets into a home,” explains designer Joe Waroquier. Though these are run-of-the-mill, everyday objects, they can take on and provide an entirely different life when displayed in the home as decor. Mismatched plates on a wall, a collection of copper pots hanging on a rod or resting on shelves in the kitchen, an old crock filled with branches—these items invite in warmth, texture, and more personality.

“I’m particularly drawn to pieces that show their age, whether it’s a little patina, a worn finish, or an imperfect glaze,” he adds. “I love mixing these finds with more contemporary furnishings because that contrast makes a room feel collected and personal rather than overly designed.”

Mirrors can be intriguing focal points of a room, or they can add dimension to a gallery wall. However you want to style them, Snyder loves finding these reflective pieces at antique malls and vintage shops.

“Purchasing something that you don’t see every day from a retailer allows this unique piece to stand out on its own, without feeling like something you see everywhere or feels too simple,” she explains.

As Snyder puts it, antique table lamps provide high visual impact with low investment. Adding one to your accent table or on a bookshelf is an easy way to make your space feel more curated while inviting in another level of ambience with the lighting.

These vintage lamps in the above space, for instance, were bought from a collector and make the primary bedroom feel much more charming and curated. And unlike antique sconces, there’s no need to hardwire these into the wall.

Besides pottery and lamps, designer Ryann Swan Hackett of Ryann Swan Design loves to find and collect other small tabletop decor for her projects. Think candleholders, vintage coffee table books, and trays—things that can layer a little history and individuality into a room or simple vignette.

“If you’re lucky,” Swan Hackett says of the vintage coffee table books, “you’ll find an old inscription inside, which gives the book a story of its own.”

In terms of midsize accent pieces, Meyer says that she has a soft spot for finding antique stools “because you can pop them into any space to add depth and dimension.” Tuck them beside an armchair in the living room, add them to an empty kitchen corner and place a plant on top, or stick one in a bathroom by the tub for somewhere to keep a drink while relaxing. And if you find an upholstered one that could use new fabric, switching it out for a pattern you love is probably worth it.

“These bring a natural warmth that can be difficult to replicate with something new, and their small size makes them fairly reasonable in price for a vintage furnishing piece,” Meyer says.

A larger piece of furniture is likely always going to be more expensive than a smaller one, but designer Yena Jung of By Yena Designs thinks vintage hutches are the versatile antiques people should pay more attention to. They come in many different shapes, styles, and sizes, and can easily make a home in any room of the house.

“A beautiful vintage hutch can add warmth, charm, and a sense of history to a space,” Jung says. “I especially love how their imperfections and natural patina make them feel collected over time.”

GTA 6 shoots to the moon and past Cyberpunk’s 2020 hype train to become most-preordered game of all time

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

GTA 6 is looking to become the biggest game of all time, and its pre-orders alone almost give it that title.

It was bound to happen anyway, but we’re happy to report GTA 6 has officially surpassed Cyberpunk 2077‘s pre-order record with over 10.3 million copies sold ahead of Nov. 19. This figure steadily continues to rise as most pre-orders happen within a week or even days of a game’s launch, so we expect it to bloat even more by this time next week.

This is according to Sensor Tower, which has a dedicated GTA 6 sales tracking tool currently reporting 10.31 million copies sold as pre-orders. In total, the game has earned over a billion USD before it even came out, and that is from the pre-orders alone, not including the horrible collector’s edition or officially licensed merch. Of this billion, 750m came from the PlayStation Store, but surprisingly, 250m were generated via Xbox. Most people seem to be purchasing the $100 edition, with 7.73 million units sold on the PS Store, and 2.58m on Xbox.

What this tells me is people are more than comfortable spending extra bucks on what they perceive as a “good deal,” which could carry incredible implications for the future of gaming, especially in the AAA sphere. If people wanna spend $100 on this, they probably would want to dish out that much money on other games, too, and I don’t think it’ll be long before the standard AAA price is adjusted from the $70-80 range to $90-100. This is going to have a horrible impact on those not using USD as their currency, with the Euro always getting a 10-15 percent worse deal, and regional pricing oftentimes making games nigh-unpurchasable in some parts of the world.

At any rate, though, these pre-order numbers signal that GTA 6 is about to have a killer first week on the market, with previous projections looking at around $5 billion by the end of its opening weekend, if not more. It is by all means going to be the biggest game ever made, even if it doesn’t turn out to be the best, and when its Online version drops, I reckon Rockstar will be noting down double-digit billions of income every quarter from this one title alone.

‘Elite’ Stars Ester Expósito and Claudia Salas Lead Carlota Pereda’s ‘The Blondes,’ Playtime Launches Sales at AFM (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Ester Expósito and Claudia Salas, the breakout stars of Netflix’s hit show “Elite,” are set to lead “The Blondes,” a darkly comic crime thriller from Spanish filmmaker Carlota Pereda (“Piggy”). Paris-based Playtime has boarded international sales and is introducing the hot project to buyers at the American Film Market.

Inspired by the true-crime story of São Paulo’s notorious “Gang of Blondes,” who kidnapped wealthy women and stole their identities, the film is described as a candy-colored “bitch noir” exploring class, privilege and appearances. Related Stories 'The Peanut Butter Falcon' Breakout Zack Gottsagen and Anna Enger Ritch to Star in 'A Reason to Stay' From Director Rippin Sindher (EXCLUSIVE) 'The Notebook' Musical Sets West End Run at Newly Renamed Judi Dench Theatre

Produced by Merry Colomer at Spain’s Morena Films, with Pilar Benito executive producing, “The Blondes” will begin shooting in March 2027. Universal Pictures International Spain will handle the Spanish release.

Expósito – whose romance with French soccer star Kylian Mbappé dominated social media chatter over the summer — plays Cristina, a ruthless blonde who transforms petty fraud into a criminal empire.

Salas stars as Marta, a working-class brunette who loses her job and discovers that a bottle of blonde hair dye and designer clothes can open doors. Alongside her best friend Pepa, she embarks on a string of scams before Cristina introduces them to express kidnappings.

Pitched as “Legally Blonde” meets “Spring Breakers,” the film expands Pereda’s short “Las Rubias,” which screened at more than 140 festivals worldwide.

“‘The Blondes’ is a heist movie turned inside out. It’s funny, sharp, and underneath hides a question about privilege, prejudice and why we think all blondes look the same,” says Pereda. “I’m thrilled ‘The Blondes’ is in their hands, a film that is both a crowd-pleaser and a film with teeth.”

Pereda’s debut feature “Piggy” premiered at Sundance in 2022, won the Méliès d’Or at Sitges and earned six Goya nominations.

Morena Films’ credits include Spanish box office hits “Champions” and “Cell 211,” as well as Asghar Farhadi’s Cannes opener “Everybody Knows.”

Playtime managing director Sébastien Beffa says the project reunites the sales outfit with Morena Films. “Her project is one of the funniest scripts we have read in a long while. It wholeheartedly embraces its genre while carrying a resolutely modern message.”

The crew includes cinematographer Rita Noriega (“Piggy”), production designer Carlota Casado, a longtime Pedro Almodóvar collaborator, and editor Verónica Callón (“Money Heist”).

Betsy James Wyeth’s Placemaking Prowess

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A wide-ranging book and exhibition reveal the ways the multi-hyphenate’s architectural-environmental projects shaped her husband Andrew Wyeth’s celebrated painting career.

The woman portrayed in Andrew Wyeth’s “Christina’s World” (1948) has become one of the most iconic figures in American art. Less familiar, perhaps, is the woman who posed for this painting: his wife, Betsy James Wyeth. Dubbed the “chief architect of the Wyeth mystique,” Betsy shaped the Wyeth brand, Andrew’s life, and art for over seven decades in undeniable ways. Though she rarely occupied the spotlight herself, her influence was everywhere: she handled his business affairs, controlled his public image, wrote books about his art, and maintained his catalogue raisonné. She also served as a creative advisor, critiquing his paintings and giving them their titles.

By Design: The Worlds of Betsy James Wyeth — a hefty and compelling new book (Rizzoli Electa) and a sprawling trio of linked exhibitions shown simultaneously at the Farnsworth Art Museum in Rockland, Maine; Brandywine Museum of Art in Chadds Ford, Pennsylvania; and Colby College Museum of Art in Waterville, Maine — pay tribute to the pair’s enduring partnership and work to credit Betsy as a key figure in her own right.

Bandai Namco Shuts Down Reflector Entertainment, Expects €21 Million in Losses

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Lauren has experience in the gaming and esports space, writing news, guides, features, reviews, lists, conducting interviews, and more. Before joining the team at DualShockers, Lauren was a Features Writer at Adventure Gamers, a Guides Writer at Gameranx, as well as contributing as a News Writer at PCInvasion and Daily

Bandai Namco has announced that it is shutting down one of its studios, Reflector Entertainment. Perhaps one of the lesser-known studios under Bandai Namco’s wing, Reflector Entertainment has yet to show its potential.

The latest and only project by Reflector Entertainment is Unknown 9: Awakening, which rolled out in October 2024. It’s an action-adventure where you step into the shoes of Haroona, who is able to enter a mysterious dimension that overlaps our own, and she can channel numerous powers on her quest.

Tripwire Interactive has been hit with layoffs for a second time this year, but it has been confirmed that work continues on Killing Floor 3 and Maneater 2.

The game is just one of many parts of the IP’s narrative universe, as there are also stories told across podcasts, comics, novels, and web series.

Bandai Namco acquired Reflector Entertainment in 2020, and even though the studio is based in Canada, Reflector joined the family over at Bandai Namco Europe. At the time of the acquisition, the studio was already working on Unknown 9: Awakening. After Unknown 9: Awakening’s weak reception among fans and critics, Reflector Entertainment announced that 18% of its staff were being laid off, just one month after the game’s launch.

Unknown 9: Awakening sits at a Top Critic Average of 62 on OpenCritic, along with just 17% of critics recommending the game. Of course, that’s less than ideal, especially with the backing of Bandai Namco, who are known for delivering some of the biggest franchises, including Dark Souls, Code Vein, Dragon Ball video games, and so much more.

The reason given for the liquidation of Reflector Entertainment was that it came as part of a continuous review into The Group’s business portfolio, and “after comprehensively considering future business plans, necessary additional investment, and the possibility of profitability and business continuation, the Company has decided to discontinue Reflector’s business, dissolve and liquidate it.”

The statement clarifies that a concrete liquidation schedule “is undecided as of now. The Company will advance the necessary procedures according to local laws and regulations, and once said procedures are completed, the liquidation will be complete.”

The matter comes with huge losses for Bandai Namco, as they expect to “post an extraordinary loss of approximately 21 million euros (approx. 3.5 billion yen) on its consolidated financial statements for the third quarter (nine months) of the fiscal year ending March 31, 2027, and thereafter.” The final figure and timing remain under review, but the impact will be eye-watering, nevertheless.

After Unknown 9: Awakening’s weak reception among fans and critics, Reflector Entertainment announced that 18% of its staff were being laid off, just one month after the game’s launch.

It’s unknown how many employees are impacted by the closure of Reflector Entertainment, and at the time of writing, Reflector Entertainment has yet to comment on its liquidation.

Sadly, Reflector Entertainment joins a long list of studios to be shut down or hit with layoffs this year. As a result of the XBOX layoffs, for example, two separate agreements to sell off the creators behind Hellblade, Ninja Theory, fell through. As a result, the studio is facing a proposed closure.

Consultation with Arkane Lyon, the team behind Dishonored and the upcoming Marvel’s Blade game, remains underway and is expected to continue through the end of the year.

Despite Ghost of Yotei's resounding success, an eighth of the team has been laid off.

NASA's secret weapon in the lunar space race may be ... procurement reform?

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

“The pace that’s occurring, and the nature of the procurements, is unprecedented.”

Senior NASA officials held a lengthy news conference last weekend, laying out a series of lunar contract awards, new contract opportunities, and mission decisions. They followed one after the other: landers, nuclear-powered rovers, scouting drones, power generation, storage, and communication satellites.

“Hopefully, we’re keeping industry on their toes trying to catch up to all the solicitations we’re doing,” said Carlos García-Galán, manager of the Moon Base Program, more than 90 minutes into the news conference. “This is what it takes to do the Moon Base in the timeline we have set forth.”

The rapid-fire announcement of awards tells us a few things about NASA’s efforts to return to the Moon and establish a durable settlement there during the next decade.

First, the agency is serious about this effort—the Artemis Moon Base program represents the most credible effort to establish a permanent outpost on another world in NASA’s history. Second, NASA is reaching out to all corners of the US commercial space industry, casting an extraordinarily wide net to reach companies that have yet to fly any hardware into orbit. Third, NASA is getting creative about how it’s buying these services, and this may matter most of all.

There was a lot packed into the announcements this weekend, so to make sense of it all, I called Mike Gold, a lawyer and former NASA associate administrator who set up the Artemis Accords, who is now the president of Redwire Space. He was at NASA at the beginning of Artemis and understands what it takes to get the government moving. He said he was blown away. During a period of seven days, Gold said, NASA put out five proposals for lunar contracts.

“The pace that’s occurring, and the nature of the procurements is unprecedented,” Gold said. “You are seeing an all-hands-on-deck approach. Big companies, small companies, new companies, old companies.”

What has impressed Gold most about NASA’s strategy since announcing the Moon Base in March, at the Ignition event, has been the agency’s evolving procurement strategy. The space agency has adopted a flexible procurement strategy similar to one used by the US Defense Advanced Research Projects Agency.

There are two components. NASA has largely used a mechanism called Broad Agency Announcements, which ask for a capability such as providing power on the surface of the Moon. This allows NASA to ask industry for ideas without being too prescriptive and levying a bunch of requirements. It’s rather like instead of telling industry precisely what it wants, NASA asks industry what it can do.

Critically, Gold said, the space agency has also used a DARPA-style preliminary proposal process. In response to a traditional procurement, interested companies would prepare a detailed proposal, often running dozens, if not hundreds, of pages. This required an extraordinary amount of time.

With the Moon Base, NASA is employing a two-step process. After NASA announces the need for a service, interested companies submit a short white paper describing their solution. After a short review, NASA identifies the concepts it wants to pursue further, and only then do companies invest the time and resources to prepare a detailed proposal.

Gold said several lunar opportunities interested Redwire, and the company submitted initial proposals in response. But it only had to prepare a full proposal for opportunities that NASA wanted to pursue, including using a variant of its roll-out solar arrays on the Moon to generate power.

“We were able to focus the lion’s share of our resources in what the agency really wanted and what we were best at,” he said.

From this shift, it seems clear that NASA is trying to move away from micromanaging every individual lunar-base subsystem and instead seeks to coordinate independently developed commercial capabilities. The space agency is sourcing them from everywhere, from major contractors like Lockheed Martin, SpaceX, Blue Origin, and Northrop Grumman to relatively new startups such as Impulse Space, ispace-US, Voyager Technologies, Quantum Space, and more.

With questions emerging about how committed SpaceX is to NASA work in the future, the space agency appears to be seeking to diversify the way it gets cargo to the Moon. For example, it is leaning into Relativity Space and its large (but unproven) Terran R rocket, as well as orbital transfer vehicles built by Impulse Space. NASA is prioritizing the creation of a whole ecosystem of potential contractors rather than relying on one or two primes.

In doing so, NASA is taking a big risk. It is attempting to accelerate its lunar program by buying services from companies whose hardware is not yet operational and, in some cases, exists only as advanced engineering designs. For NASA, ultimately, it is a high-risk, high-reward approach. But in a demanding contest with China to reach and establish settlements on the Moon, NASA’s post-Apollo timidity is no longer tenable.

So how will NASA pay for all of the new stuff? Some of the funding is being repurposed from the previously shuttered Lunar Gateway program, NASA Administrator Jared Isaacman said. For example, as part of the “One Big Beautiful Bill” act passed in 2025, NASA received $2.6 billion for the Gateway. Some of the funding was already appropriated through the agency’s Commercial Lunar Payload Services Program.

Moreover, Isaacman said, some of these contracts are relatively small and will enable NASA to invest more down the road in successful services.

“By doing a lot of littles, and learning from our approach and also putting some good wins on the board, we’re demonstrating that we can be very efficient and responsible with taxpayer dollars,” he said. With this approach, he added, “You give future administrations the option of how they can dial up or down missions. Some of these missions in the, call it, $200 million range between launch, lander, and payload; potentially, you can add those or drop those inside a $25 billion approximate budget per year without putting the entire program at risk.”

Ars Technica has been separating the signal from the noise for over 25 years. With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Thomas Tuchel keeps laser focus on ‘unfinished business’ with England

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Tuchel did not insert release clause into England deal

Coach ‘open to everything’ including staying after Euros

Thomas Tuchel will not be going anywhere before Euro 2028. Mauricio Pochettino is expected to leave his role as the USA men’s head coach to replace Michael Carrick at Manchester United, but Tuchel has not lost his passion for international football and has revealed he did not insert a release clause when he extended his deal as England’s manager this year.

It will not matter whether there is an approach from a big club before the next tournament. United can pay about £7m to get Pochettino out of his deal, but the Football Association does not have to worry about being forced to let Tuchel go. “There’s no intention [of leaving],” Tuchel said. “I signed the contract. I want to stay until the Euros. I want to do the Euros. We have unfinished business.”

Tuchel, a Champions League winner with Chelsea in 2021, did not rule out a return to club management one day. He admitted to missing “the 50 matches” a season, “these two minutes when everything is done, before the whistle goes … it’s like the best two minutes”. But while he loves the daily grind, he added there were downsides to playing every four days and there was a twinkle in his eye when he was asked about potentially staying with England beyond the European Championship.

“I am open to everything,” he said, perhaps reflecting that it is not so long ago that people were calling for his head after England’s World Cup semi-final defeat by Argentina.

The mood has improved. Tuchel sounds energised after England’s bright start to their Nations League campaign. His contract contains a performance-related clause that would have allowed the FA to sack him had England underperformed at the World Cup, but Tuchel has support from Mark Bullingham, the chief executive, and Dan Ashworth, the chief football officer.

Ashworth and Bullingham reinforced the message in person during the summer. “They made it very early in the World Cup,” Tuchel said, explaining that the backing came while the sacking clause remained active. “They just made it very clear and it comes from a place of trust, and that’s what you look for as a coach.

“I love my players. I love my staff and I have bosses who trust me. I know how to appreciate it a lot. I miss my players, and I miss my team, and I miss the 50 matches that I’m not involved in a year. But I’m desperate to win the Nations League and to try to win the Euros.”

Tuchel, who is preparing for the Nations League game at home against Croatia on Thursday night, is thinking ahead. He is gearing up for a European Championship staged mainly in England, who would also like to be chosen to host the Nations League finals next summer. “To have it here would be exciting,” Tuchel said. “First of all, we need to reach the final.”

England need one point from their final two games in Group A3 to finish in the top two and qualify for the quarter-finals in March. Victory against Croatia would give them a chance of finishing top when they visit Spain on Sunday. The Croatia game will not merely be a celebration of Harry Kane’s Ballon d’Or. Wembley will watch the captain break the England men’s appearance record, but other issues are at play.

Tuchel needs Jude Bellingham to be careful given that England’s No 10 is on a booking and at risk of missing the Spain game through suspension. There are unknowns in central defence after Marc Guéhi’s withdrawal. Ezri Konsa is unavailable against Croatia and targeting a return against Spain. Tuchel, who added James Hill and Dan Burn to his squad this week, will use a new partnership against Slaven Bilic’s side.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

It is likely to be Jarell Quansah as the right centre-back and Jarrad Branthwaite could miss out to Burn on the left. “Jarrad is doing very well but it’s a very different style of play for him from Everton towards us,” Tuchel said. “Dan Burn is more used to the style with Newcastle from the last two seasons where they followed in a high press.”

Burn led the team out when England trained on Wednesday. Among the group are exciting newcomers who inevitably command the attention. Tuchel has Rio Ngumoha and Josh King in attack. He is the first senior manager to work with the Bellingham brothers. Jobe Bellingham, who has impressed with his physicality and calm on the ball, plays a deeper midfield role than Jude. Jobe is likely to start on the bench.

Tuchel wants to reconnect with the values from the previous camp. It featured England impressing in a narrow defeat by Spain, before they crushed Croatia 7-0 in Rijeka. One big highlight from the Croatia win was Alex Scott’s performance alongside Elliot Anderson in midfield.

Scott, who is out with a thigh injury, can challenge Declan Rice. The competition for places is fierce. Rice is back after missing the last camp. Tuchel said the Arsenal midfielder remained crucial.

“Declan understands the game like he’s almost a coach on the field,” Tuchel said, but a new identity is forming. It was interesting that Tuchel mentioned the young Chelsea defender Josh Acheampong, whose first call-up may not be far away. It seems Tuchel 2.0 is taking shape.

Its custom ASIC chip pulls a tenfold capacity gain forward to satellite 34, a shift that matters more to long-term holders than the launch timeline alone.

On its third-quarter 2026 business update call, held on Nov. 9, 2026, AST SpaceMobile (ASTS -8.67%) announced plans to build its own reusable rocket alongside securing $1 billion in non-dilutive Japanese government funding. Gaining control over launch schedules and securing non-dilutive capital significantly reduce execution risks for the satellite network build-out. Three things management said stand out.

AST SpaceMobile is developing a 20-ton reusable rocket called Blacklift to lower launch expenses and eliminate reliance on third-party launch schedules. Reusable rockets give the business owner economic benefits and direct control over its orbital deployment schedule starting in late 2028.

The company is pulling forward its next-generation application-specific integrated circuit (ASIC) -- a custom microchip designed for high-speed signal processing -- to BlueBird satellite 34. This hardware shift increases data throughput tenfold while keeping monthly factory output on track for six satellites in December 2026.

International governments want national control over satellite communications, creating opportunities for joint ventures and dedicated regional constellations. Japan's $1 billion subsidy demonstrates how sovereign demand provides capital without diluting existing shareholder equity.

Commercial deployment and Space Force contracts are accelerating. Proprietary phased arrays and $3.2 billion in cash provide key advantages as management tracks production throughput.

Long-term growth depends on manufacturing execution and launch control.

Investing - Theory, News &amp; General • Re: Box Spreads as Loans - Interactive Brokers IBKR - 2021 [and later]

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →



I spent significant time re-thinking this and examining the sources and papers, and respectfully, I think you are wrong.

You're again just leaving a thread filled with long confusing posts which won't help anybody trying to learn. You should stop IMO, though up to you.

I never once mentioned the term 'repo rate' except in response to your confusing source. I'll just lay it out one more time for future reference of people looking at the thread.

The index call price is per B-S formula N(d1)*S*e^-(d*t)-N(d2)*K*e^-(r*t)
'r' is often called 'the risk free rate' but is actually the rate at which the 'market clearing participant' can finance the physical stock basket of stocks using them as collateral. So it's somewhat specific to that underlying (the SPX in this case) and market. It doesn't need to be equal to what individual investors are (naturally) used to thinking of as the 'risk free rate', the T-bill yield, nor somewhat equivalent rates they're less familiar with (again for good reason) like the SOFR swap rate. 'r' for box spread 'is what it is' and we just need to evaluate it if we're borrowing (no credit risk to us) compared to other alternatives, also considering tax (the 'interest' will manifest as a Section 1256 mark to market capital loss, again for SPX options, 60% long term/40% short term regardless of holding period, plus whatever state tax). If instead we're investing via box spread, we need to consider the actual credit risk we're taking (risk of collapse of the broker dealer *and* the options exchange) v how much higher the rate is than T-bill (again after tax, T-bill ordinary income Fed but no state tax).

'd' is the dividend yield of the index.

The word 'repo' hasn't appeared in this or my other direct explanations and needn't. Again your source IMO is inherently confusing in adopting this term as a general monicker for the rate discounting the underlying, because the term 'repo rate' much more often refers to the rate at which *US treasuries* can be financed using those treasuries as collateral which has nothing directly to do with prices of SPX box spreads. And in the SPX case again, the discount rate attached to 'S' is the *dividend yield*, there's no reason to call it by any other more general name.
I'm sorry that you find my posts confusing. I carefully read dozens of quantitative finance sources that are linked from the Google results. I stand by my interpretation, which is also very explicitly stated in the screenshots, the actual quant finance sources of which I read and verified:
The expected dividend d, or often designated as q, along with other cash flows associated with holding the underlying, go into the term that contains d, q, rrepo, or rf - rrepo (depending on the formulation). The inputs in this term represent the net cost of holding the underlying in the delta hedged portfolio that is used in the derivation of the Black-Scholes formula.
The risk-free rate associated with the dollar value of the options spread is in the term that contains rf only, and represents the financing rate of dollars borrowed or invested.

In addition to what is explicitly said in the screenshots, and in all the sources that I read and verified, this also makes intuitively sense (to me at least): Only the former term, that contains the underlying process (S), depends explicitly on the delta exposure of the option, while the latter depends heavily on the strike prices selected (K), and therefore on the dollars borrowed or invested.

If you still find this confusing, and don't cite evidence or elaborate on reasoning to the contrary, then we simply have to agree to disagree at this point.

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

With limited exception, the mid-size company I work for will not contract with sole proprietors who do not have an LLC. It is part of our audit defense to avoid mis- and re-classification of contractors vs. employees.
Your mid-size company needs to hire better accountants and/or lawyers - the business structure of a contractor will have zero impact on IRS classification and mean nothing in an audit.
The issue is not the IRS, but the state (e.g. California) coming down on the client for not following employment law.

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: HSA eligible or not HSA eligible ?

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

There where big changes to HSA eligibility in the OBBB Act.

Currently any Bronze or Catastrophic plan is HSA eligible, even if it has copays or other benefits before the deductible. This might make such plans more attractive than a traditional "HSA" plan (which still exist), but in some cases the deductible and OOP can be even higher than what would qualify as a HDHP, because HDHP plan's deductibles are limited by statue, while Bronze plans (ex-HDHP) are only limited by actuarial value and not by deductible directly.

So, long story short, it might be worth looking into this plan a bit more.
This is an employer plan and unlikely to qualify. The bronze rule only works for plans that are offered on the exchange.

Bessent reportedly moved to military base over Iranian threats on his life

📅 2026-11-11  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

U.S. Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent was reportedly moved from his Washington, D.C., home to a military base due to Iranian threats on his life.

The New York Times, citing officials familiar with the matter, said that U.S. officials had concluded Iranian plotting against current and former American officials had reached a more advanced stage.

Separately, Reuters reported that the severity of the threats against Bessent was not immediately clear.

Bessent has led "Operation Economic Outcast," a Treasury campaign launched in August to isolate Iran economically through sanctions and other financial measures, in addition to the U.S. military actions.

The Times reported that Bessent's role in that campaign has made him one of the Trump administration officials Iran most wanted to target.

The move comes as the Department of Justice Department said late Tuesday that three Iranian nationals had been indicted on terrorism-related charges.

The department said the three were allegedly involved in nearly 20 attacks and attempted attacks across Europe and the United States.

The alleged plots included a September 2026 attack targeting RAF Fairford in the U.K., where U.S. military personnel were stationed.

"Anyone who plans a terrorist plot on a military base housing American servicemembers will be prosecuted by the United States government," Attorney General Todd Blanche said.

Earlier in October, British Prime Minister Andy Burnham said Iran was involved in the incident at RAF Fairford that led to the arrest of five men on suspicion of explosives and terrorism offences.

Iranian Foreign Minister Abbas Araghchi then rejected the allegation, writing on X that Burnham was "barking up the wrong tree."

RAF Fairford, in Gloucestershire, is a key U.S. Air Force operating location in Europe and has supported deployments of American strategic bombers and other aircrafts.

Separately, U.S. Secretary of State Marco Rubio reportedly said military action against Iran may not be necessary if economic sanctions and Washington's naval blockade are working.

On Monday, President Donald Trump said on Truth Social that "Qatar, Saudi Arabia, UAE, Bahrain, Kuwait, and others, would prefer the status quo, in other words, a continuation of the Greatest Naval Blockade in History."

"We are seeing positive momentum toward an end to this Conflict," Trump added.

Asked Tuesday why he would not simply declare an end to the Iran war, Trump said he wanted to ensure U.S. allies in the region were "not hurt" and that there was a "long term solution.

However, Iranian officials have denied that negotiations are underway. Security chief Mohsen Rezaei said claims that talks were continuing were aimed at "easing economic pressures and reducing oil prices."

Brent and West Texas Intermediate crude prices edged higher during Wednesday's Asia trading session, at about $92 and $86 per barrel, respectively.

EU Seeks Experts to Help Set Tokenisation Standards as Financial Markets Move Onchain

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: European authorities are inviting nominations for a new Tokenisation Standards Group, with applications due on November 27. The effort is about practical coordination across the financial system, not a new approval for cryptocurrency tokens or a promise that all tokenized assets will be regulated alike.

Europe is trying to solve a problem that has followed tokenized finance from pilot projects into real markets: moving an asset onto a blockchain is only part of the job. Trading, ownership records, custody, corporate actions, payments, and reporting still need to work together.

In a November 9 announcement, the European Securities and Markets Authority said the EU was seeking nominations for a European Tokenisation Standards Group. The deadline for candidates is November 27, 2026, meaning the process remains in its membership-building stage.

A token might provide a fast record of a transfer, but its legal and operational meaning depends on the system behind it. If the asset represents a bond, someone must still ensure that the person receiving it has enforceable rights, that payments reach the correct holder, and that the records remain accurate when a key is lost or a transaction is reversed under applicable law.

Those tasks become harder when different issuers, custodians and trading venues use incompatible methods. Common standards can reduce integration work and make it easier for supervised firms to compare and connect systems. They do not eliminate risk, but they can help clarify who is responsible when something goes wrong.

That is why a coordination effort deserves attention even if it does not announce a flashy new chain or a major token launch. The goal is to make the plumbing of tokenized markets more predictable.

The NYSE’s proposed tokenized-securities venue is an example of how conventional-market institutions are exploring blockchain-based records while maintaining established securities functions. It is a different jurisdiction and project, but it points to the same practical issue: tokenization has to coexist with financial-market rules and infrastructure.

At the same time, Securitize’s work to expand tokenized assets across networks shows why interoperability matters to firms operating in more than one technical environment. A tokenization service that works on several networks can still face fragmented onboarding, settlement and compliance processes.

For European banks, asset managers and infrastructure operators, the payoff from consistent standards would be less duplicated work and more confidence in how instruments move between systems. Whether those efficiencies materialize depends on the resulting proposals and industry adoption.

This announcement should not be read as the EU approving all forms of real-world asset tokens, replacing securities law, or selecting a particular blockchain as a winner. Nor does a call for nominations itself establish binding technical requirements. It is an early process intended to bring stakeholders into the conversation.

Different asset classes could continue to require different handling. A tokenized money-market fund, for instance, has obligations that a privately issued collectible does not. Regulation, transfer restrictions and investor eligibility remain important regardless of how assets are represented.

November 27 is the immediate date to watch. After nominations are considered, the composition of the group and the work it chooses to prioritize will give a clearer indication of what practical standards could emerge.

The significance for crypto markets is less about speculative prices and more about infrastructure. If regulators and financial firms can agree on reliable operating conventions, tokenized finance may have a better chance of expanding beyond isolated tests and into services that ordinary institutions actually use.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/payers/positive-medicaid-signals-open-enrollment-trends-recapping-major-payers-q3-results" hreflang="en">Positive Medicaid signals, open enrollment trends: Recapping major payers' Q3 results</a>

📅 2026-11-10  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →
Positive Medicaid signals, open enrollment trends: Recapping major payers' Q3 results" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

While medical cost trend remains a pressure point for health insurers, leading companies cited some positive trends, too, heading into the final months of 2026.

UnitedHealth Group was once again the most profitable company among payers in the quarter, bringing in $4.1 billion in profit for Q3. That's up from a $2.3 billion haul in the prior-year quarter.

Through the first nine months of the year, profits were $15.9 billion at UHG, rising from $12 billion a year ago.

The healthcare giant has been in the midst of a critical financial turnaround since mid-2025, and in the most recent quarter, it highlighted some of the progress that it's logged over the past year at one of its biggest pain centers: Optum Health.

When the company posted an uncharacteristically poor quarter to start 2025, Optum Health was cited as one of the main pressure points. During its most recent call, Optum CEO Patrick Conway, M.D., said that the transition centered on a "continued return to value-based care principles" that sit at the core of Optum Health.

UnitedHealth also led the way on revenue in Q3, posting $112.7 billion. That's down slightly from $113.2 billion the company, reported in the third quarter of 2025.

CVS Health had the second-highest profit and revenue in Q3, bringing in $2.2 billion in earnings for the quarter. This marks a significant reversal from the prior-year quarter, when the company posted a nearly $4 billion loss.

CVS' revenue in the quarter was $108.4 billion, increasing from $102.9 billion in the third quarter of 2025.

The company's financial performance wasn't what dominated the headlines in Q3, however, as CVS shocked the industry in announcing plans to split its pharmacy business off and form two companies. The break-up is expected to close in the first half of 2028, and would establish CVS Pharmacy as its own company while Aetna and Caremark would remain together as CVS Health.

CVS CEO David Joyner said that the shift represents "a structure and plan that are built to win and represent the best path to create value over the long term."

The Cigna Group and Elevance Health were on par with one another in Q3 on profits, both earning $1.4 billion. Cigna wins out on revenue, though, with $73.7 billion, while Elevance reported $51.4 billion.

By comparison, Cigna posted $1.9 billion in profit and $69.7 billion in revenue in Q3 2025.

Core to the third-quarter conversation was the company's newly-unveiled Lead to One healthcare transformation strategy, which puts an emphasis on building a personalized experience for its members and customers.

Cigna said it plans to lean on all the segments under its umbrella to drive toward meeting individual member needs.

Elevance Health, meanwhile, increased its profit slightly year over year from $1.2 billion in Q3 2025. Revenues in the prior-year quarter were $50.7 million.

The company was one of several insurers in the quarter that noted positive signs in its Medicaid plans. While its leaders told investors that 2026 remains a "trough year" for its Medicaid business, improvements are likely in the cards for 2027.

That trend is supported by state rate setting that more fully accounts for the rising cost of care and the acuity of people enrolled in Medicaid, executives said. The member mix is only expected to increase in acuity as states roll out work requirements at the beginning of this year.

Similarly, Centene CEO Sarah London said the company sees an opportunity for "long-term growth" in the Medicaid market as part of its Q3 report. She noted similar trends around rate setting from states.

London added that the insurer has made significant progress on focus areas for improvement, such as behavioral health.

Centene posted a $71 million loss in the quarter, slimming its losses significantly from $6.6 billion in the prior-year quarter. That massive loss a year ago was backstopped by a one-time impairment charge of more than $6 billion.

Revenues in Q3 were $50.6 billion, up slightly from $49.7 billion in the third quarter of 2025.

Humana offered color on early signals in the Medicare annual enrollment period, which suggests that the insurer is poised for further growth in Medicare Advantage for next year. CEO Jim Rechtin said sign-ups through the early parts of the window indicate 2027 membership growth.

The insurer has had momentum around new sign-ups and has also done well in recapturing enrollments that were impacted by market exits, Rechtin said.

Humana posted a $162 million profit in the third quarter, down from $195 million a year ago. Revenues were $41.15 billion, growing from $32.6 billion in the prior-year quarter.

Better Buy Right Now: Cardano vs. Solana

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Both of these coins showed a lot of promise in 2021, but only one has delivered on it.

Cardano (ADA +1.03%) and Solana (SOL -1.27%) are both major cryptocurrencies, yet in my view, only one of them is worth owning. With that said, both coins have been a bit frustrating to hold; as of Nov. 6, Solana is 69% below its all-time high set in early 2025, while Cardano's price is 94% below its peak set in late 2021.

So which of these two bruised blockchains is better positioned to perform better over the next few years?

In crypto, a network's transaction fees are important to track because they're a clear sign of how much users are willing to pay to use the network.

For October, Solana brought in $23.1 million in fees, which is typical for it, and Cardano brought in $141,330, which, for it, is unusually high for 2026. Furthermore, Solana's per-transaction fees are typically much lower than Cardano's. A basic Solana transfer costs about $0.0005 as of Nov. 6, while a simple Cardano transfer costs roughly $0.03 and is also tens of seconds slower.

Additionally, in late July 2026, Solana boosted its transaction throughput by increasing each block's computing capacity by 66%, and then, on Oct. 21, its latest Alpenglow upgrade reduced transaction finalization time from 12.8 seconds to roughly 150 milliseconds. That speed makes Solana highly suitable for processing payments on a massive scale.

And that's part of the reason why, as of October, the network has a new collaboration with Samsung, which, starting Nov. 5, lets U.S. users on 82 million compatible Galaxy devices send the USDC stablecoin to each other, to outside wallets, and to bank accounts in more than 60 countries through Samsung Wallet, with Solana as a network partner. In contrast, Cardano has no collaborations or partnerships on Samsung's scale.

Another domain where Solana is far ahead is in its tokenized real-world asset base.

It currently holds $4.9 billion in tokenized real-world assets, including stocks, bonds, and funds recorded as blockchain tokens. Cardano doesn't even have a presence in that market, which means it'll miss out on the growth stemming from financial institutions seeking to manage their holdings via tokenization over the coming years.

Cardano does have a small decentralized finance (DeFi) ecosystem, which holds $54.1 million in total value locked (TVL), or deposited capital. Solana's DeFi ecosystem has $5.6 billion in TVL. Worse yet, Cardano's ecosystem of blockchain applications has shrunk by around 21% in just three months.

Solana is by far the better coin to buy compared to Cardano.

Aside from the diverging trajectories of their ecosystems, Cardano does not appear to have a clear idea of how to arrest its ongoing decline. Nor does it have any real areas of strength to consolidate around; it lacks on-chain capital to power ecosystem growth. That could well change in the coming years, but it doesn't seem probable -- the current state of affairs is not new for the network, and there still isn't any compelling reason for anyone to add its coin to a diversified crypto portfolio.

Solana, on the other hand, is still not exactly a safe purchase, even if it's preferable to Cardano.

While Cardano's inroads into asset tokenization are accelerating its exposure to growth, and its deals with outside companies to find more uses for its rails are bullish, there still isn't a strong link between its network activity and the returns that holders actually see from buying the coin. That could also change in the coming years, and it probably will -- but one big risk is that it won't.

So, be aware that Solana, the blockchain, could win big without Solana, the coin, winning much at all, even if it's highly likely to do vastly better than Cardano.

Alex Carchidi has positions in Solana. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Solana. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Brent crude falls below $90 for first time since August on Iran war de-escalation hopes

📅 2026-11-10  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

International oil benchmark Brent Crude fell below $90 a barrel for the first time since August on Tuesday as comments from U.S. President Donald Trump fueled hopes for an end to Washington's nearly nine-month conflict with Iran.

Brent, the price gauge for around 80% of global crude, was 2.65% lower at $90.14 at 5:33 a.m. ET after contracts with January expiry dipped below $90 earlier in the morning.

U.S. WTI Crude was down 2.4% at $84.78 a barrel.

Trump said on Monday that the war with Iran could end "fairly soon" and that Tehran was "talking very politely" with Washington.

"We're seeing momentum toward the end of this conflict. I think it's going to be ending fairly soon," he said at the White House following a meeting with Qatar's prime minister, Sheikh Mohammed bin Abdulrahman Al Thani. "Iran militarily is effectively over," Trump told reporters.

Trump also said in a Truth Social post that Qatar, Saudi Arabia, the UAE, Bahrain and Kuwait would "prefer the status quo," namely maintaining a naval blockade against Iran and preventing the country from using ground routes to transport advanced weapons and other materials.

Crude oil prices have been highly volatile this year, proving sensitive to any perceived shift in direction in the U.S. and Israel's war with Iran.

Brent Crude started the year around $60 a barrel before soaring as high as $130 a barrel as military strikes escalated in March and April. Prices retreated in June and July before climbing back to those levels as the conflict increasingly appeared entrenched.

Crude oil flows through the key Strait of Hormuz waterway have largely recovered. But consumers have suffered from resulting volatility in energy markets, as wider global conflicts — including Houthi attacks on Saudi Arabia and the Russia-Ukraine war — exacerbate a shortage of refining capacity, sending prices of diesel, gasoline and jet fuel soaring.

Trump and Vice President JD Vance have both acknowledged that public disapproval of the Iran war and high energy prices played a role in the Republicans' punishing result in the U.S. midterms last week.

While officials on both sides have continued to trade hostile words, particularly as they debate control of the Strait of Hormuz, the U.S. had largely held back on military strikes since September, with Trump saying in early October they would not resume until after the midterms.

The U.S. carried out what Trump called "minor strikes" on Iran after reports that two missiles struck Qeshm Island, located near the Strait of Hormuz off the country's southern coast.

Analysts were divided on how the U.S. administration would approach the Iran conflict after the Democrats' resounding midterm victories, with some viewing escalation as a strong possibility despite the initial market reaction suggesting hopes of a deal.

"The market has continued to unwind some of the geopolitical risk premium built into prices in recent weeks as expectations grow that the conflict between the US and Iran could move towards de-escalation," ING strategists Ewa Manthey and Warren Patterson said in a note Tuesday.

"Signs that regional oil flows are recovering have added to the downward pressure... Reports that Iran may be softening its position on control of the Strait of Hormuz have also reduced concerns over a prolonged disruption to one of the world's most important oil transit routes."

"For now, the return of more Gulf barrels and the prospect of easing tensions are taking some of the geopolitical premium out of crude. However, with regional negotiations still uncertain and infrastructure risks persisting, we expect oil prices to remain sensitive to developments in the Middle East."

Fereidun Fesharaki, chairman of energy consultancy FGE, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" on Tuesday that, despite market moves, he still did not see any prospects of a peace deal between the U.S. and Iran.

"The difference between the two sides is so wide, it cannot be bridged. Most importantly, the U.S. wants all the nuclear material, enriched material, to be transferred out of Iran, and there is no way that the Iranian regime would agree to this, and there is no way the Iranian regime would agree to let the Strait of Hormuz be open for a free flow of oil the way it was before the war," Fesharaki said.

"So, with these two in between, I just can't see a deal being made.

"Trump has to decide. Either he says, 'I declare victory and walk away,' or he goes to war. If he goes to war, he's got only a window of two weeks from today for himself and for [Israeli Prime Minister] Netanyahu, or there will be no war at all on a long-term basis," Fesharaki added.

Netanyahu's political party failed to win a majority in Israel's election last month, with rival Gadi Eisenkot, the former chief of the Israel Defense Forces, officially tasked with forming a government on Sunday.

— CNBC's Anniek Bao contributed to this story.

Rui Pinto’s lawyers in talks with UK authorities over cooperation deal for whistleblower

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

He is willing to assist in exchange for protection

Rui Pinto’s lawyers are in talks with British authorities over a potential cooperation agreement under which the Manchester City whistleblower could help to expose serious financial and sporting crime in the UK.

According to information obtained by the Guardian, the Serious Fraud Office (SFO) has contacted Pinto after it emerged he was willing to assist British authorities with their investigations in exchange for protection.

William Bourdon, Pinto’s lawyer, who has represented whistleblowers such as Edward Snowden and Julian Assange, told the Guardian contact had been established. “I can confirm that the process is under way,” he said, without providing further details.

If an agreement is reached, the Football Leaks founder could become a significant source of information in investigations into financial crime involving football, law firms and powerful business figures. Between 2015 and 2019, Pinto collected tens of millions of files containing sensitive information. He may be able to assist the SFO in identifying potential tax offences and other irregularities that would otherwise have gone undetected.

The development comes at a significant moment for the 38-year-old Pinto, with his future under Portugal’s witness protection programme uncertain.

Pinto rose to prominence by exposing some of the football industry’s most closely guarded secrets. These included Cristiano Ronaldo’s tax affairs, plans by Europe’s leading clubs to establish a breakaway European Super League and a series of controversial financial arrangements. His revelations triggered investigations and contributed to legal proceedings involving clubs and players across several countries.

More recently, documents collected and leaked by the whistleblower became relevant to the Premier League’s investigation into Manchester City, which resulted in the club being found in breach of financial rules.

The information gathered by Pinto is in the possession of French authorities, who previously sought to include him in a witness protection programme. His arrest by Portuguese police in Hungary in January 2019 disrupted those plans, although France has maintained contact with him. Between his arrest in Hungary and his extradition to Portugal, French authorities travelled to Budapest and copied the contents of the electronic devices in his possession.

Pinto was informed on 30 September, just as his name had made headlines again after the City ruling, that he would no longer be protected by the Portuguese authorities after six years under the witness protection programme. Although the authorities maintained there was no threat to his life, he was advised not to express opinions on controversial issues, to avoid crowded places and to limit the time spent with family members.

The best of our sports journalism from the past seven days and a heads-up on the weekend’s action

Pinto was given 10 days’ warning, even though a decision had been taken in July, and the ruling’s implementation was suspended after it was established he had not been given an opportunity to mount a challenge or appeal. He then appealed and is awaiting a final ruling.

In 2023 Pinto was given a suspended four-year prison sentence in Portugal for attempted extortion and cybercrime offences. He was acquitted in a second case involving similar charges two years later. The public prosecutor’s office and Benfica, one of the clubs hacked by Pinto, appealed against the ruling. A decision from the higher court is awaited.

Supporters regard Pinto as a whistleblower who cast light on corruption, whereas detractors argue that his tactics breached legal standards and moral principles. His disclosures reached far beyond football. Details involving magistrates, legal professionals and public officials were made public and he served as the whistleblower behind Luanda Leaks, which exposed the inner workings of the business empire of Africa’s richest woman, Isabel dos Santos. She denied corruption allegations.

Jimmy Stewart’s New Biopic Struggles at the Box Office Despite 96% Audience Score

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. He also writes obsessively about the box office, charting the many hits and misses that are released weekly, and how their commercial performance shapes public perception. In his time at Collider, he has also helped drive diversity by writing stories about the multiple Indian film industries, with a goal of introducing audiences to a whole new world of cinema.

This past weekend coincidentally saw two international movies debut in the top five at the domestic box office, while two films about filmmaking claimed lower spots on the leaderboard. The two international movies — Japan's Godzilla Minus Zero and India's Ramayana: Rise of a Legend — bookended the top-five list. Meanwhile, the two films about filmmaking delivered vastly different performances. I Play Rocky, the inspirational drama about Sylvester Stallone's rags-to-riches story, found success in limited release, while a biopic of another Hollywood icon delivered a lukewarm debut.

I Play Rocky is being viewed as an Oscar contender thanks to incredible reviews. Starring Anthony Ippolito as Stallone, the film holds a "Certified Fresh" 89% score on the aggregator website Rotten Tomatoes. The site's consensus reads, "A feel-good celebration of the underdog who created one of cinema's greatest underdogs, I Play Rocky may not be as powerful as the original article but packs considerable punch thanks to Anthony Ippolito's uncanny embodiment of Sly Stallone." The movie grossed nearly $350,000 from just seven locations, for an incredible per-theater average of nearly $50,000.

Meanwhile, a biopic of the legendary Jimmy Stewart underperformed in nearly 1,500 theaters nationwide, debuting outside the top 10. Titled Jimmy, the movie holds a 63% critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes, but the film's audience score on the aggregator website is sitting at a near-perfect 96%. While this may not do much for the film during its box-office run, it will certainly come in handy when the movie lands on the PVOD market.

Four icons and four completely different ways of walking into trouble. This is the one you would have been out there, why it fits, and what it would have cost you.

You have never explained yourself and you have never needed to, because people work out fairly quickly that you are the most capable person in the room without you ever having to mention it.

You are the funniest person in most rooms and the most loyal person in all of them, and you would be genuinely insulted if somebody suggested those two things were in any tension.

You never wanted this job, and you took it anyway because the alternative was standing there watching somebody less careful do it badly, which is the most Western reason there has ever been.

Nobody has ever successfully talked you out of something you had already decided was right, and a long line of grown adults has found that out the hard way.

However, that's a while away. Jimmy grossed only around $2 million in its first three days, falling short of the $6 million earned by fellow new release Wild Horse Nine, which is in 400 fewer theaters. The domestic box-office leaderboard was topped by Godzilla Minus Zero, which grossed roughly $32 million over the weekend.

Jimmy stars Riverdale alum KJ Apa as the Hollywood icon, who headlined classics such as Vertigo, It's a Wonderful Life, and The Man Who Shot Liberty Valance, among others. The movie also features Jason Alexander, Neal McDonough, and Rob Riggle. Produced on a reported budget of $14 million, Jimmy focuses on Stewart's life and career when, as an Oscar winner, he enlisted in the army to fight in World War II and returned to America to star in It's a Wonderful Life. Another biopic aimed at a similar audience — Hershey — will be released theatrically next week.

2 Dividend Kings Worth Buying and 1 Whose Streak Has Become a Liability

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Dividend Kings typically provide growing streams of reliable income, but investors must watch the trajectory of these businesses.

Investors looking for income vehicles often seek stocks with long track records of payout hikes. Although companies have the freedom to adjust dividend payments at will, abandoning a decades-long streak of periodic dividend hikes can undermine confidence in a stock, so the payout increases tend to continue.

This is especially true of Dividend Kings, or companies that have a streak of annual payout hikes of 50 years or longer. Knowing that, two stocks are in a particularly strong position to continue increasing their payouts, while one stock may leave investors questioning whether the dividend increases can continue without undermining the company's growth.

PepsiCo (PEP +0.38%) has increased its dividend for 54 straight years. In May, the latest increase hiked its annual dividend to $5.92 per share. A combination of those payout hikes and stock price declines has taken its dividend yield to 4.1%, almost quadruple the S&P 500 average of 1.1%.

However, the most compelling reason to buy PepsiCo is arguably the company's transformation. PepsiCo encompasses numerous beverage and food brands in addition to its flagship cola, but over the last few years, sales have stagnated as consumers sought healthier alternatives.

PepsiCo responded by acquiring new brands and changing the ingredients in some of its products, and that approach seems to have worked. In the third quarter of fiscal 2026 (ended Sept. 5), revenue of $25 billion climbed by 6% from the same prior-year period. Also, its over $3 billion in net income surpassed the year-ago level of $2.6 billion and exceeded the $2 billion quarterly cost of its payout.

Investors have only recently begun to see a turnaround, but it is likely not too late to buy considering its 18 P/E ratio. Given that low valuation, now might be a great time to buy before more investors discover PepsiCo's attractive value proposition.

McDonald's (MCD +1.78%) is the market's newest Dividend King. In September, it approved its 50th consecutive annual payout hike, taking the yearly dividend to $7.72 per share. With that increase, investors now earn a 2.7% dividend yield.

McDonald's has succeeded with its unique approach to franchising, which makes its stock more recession-resistant. Franchises are approximately 95% of all McDonald's restaurants and provide steady cash flows that are only minimally affected by economic cycles.

In addition to start-up fees, the company owns the real estate for franchise stores, making rent a significant source of revenue. Additionally, franchises pay between 4% and 5% of their annual gross sales to the company, along with various other fees.

Such payments helped Q3 revenue grow to $7.2 billion, a 1% increase from year-ago levels. Falling expenses meant the $2.5 billion in net income increased by 9%. Additionally, its profits were well above the $1.3 billion in quarterly dividend costs, making it more likely to maintain its Dividend King status over time.

Investors will also like that its current 22 P/E ratio is well below its five-year average of 26, suggesting it sells at a discount. Given the low earnings multiple and the stability of its business, owning this stock should continue to deliver profits for investors.

Even among Dividend Kings, Universal Corporation (UVV -1.78%) gets relatively little attention despite its dividend yield. Its payout has risen for 56 straight years, and at $3.32 per share annually, it yields an astounding 8.4%! Unfortunately, such yields often signal a stock's struggles rather than an excellent buying opportunity, and indeed, that appears to be the case with Universal.

The problem appears to stem from the company's main revenue source. Although it operates a business providing fruit- and vegetable-based ingredients, its main business is growing tobacco. The tobacco industry successfully mitigated the falling popularity of its main product for decades, but Universal's financials may leave investors wondering whether the company can continue to generate the growth it needs to sustain its Dividend King status.

In the second quarter of fiscal 2027 (ended Sept. 30), its revenue of $622 million fell 18% from year-ago levels. Universal earned $13 million in fiscal Q2, but that was well below the $34 million profit in the year-ago quarter. That is also less than the approximate $20 million the company spends on its dividend each quarter.

Additionally, the declining revenue is not a one-time event, as analysts forecast lower revenue for at least the next two quarters. Hence, unless Universal's management can find a way to turn the company around, it may have no choice but to eventually abandon its Dividend King status.

Will Healy has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool recommends the following options: long January 2028 $320 calls on McDonald's and short January 2028 $340 calls on McDonald's. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

GdS: The full story of Milan and Inter’s record-breaking €650m deal with Generali

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

More details continue to emerge regarding the stadium naming rights deal between AC Milan, Inter and Generali.

La Gazzetta dello Sport write that even before it’s even been built, the new home of Milan and Inter is already the place to be. It’s no coincidence that Generali – the insurance giant – has decided to put its stamp on the façades, as was revealed last month at the presentation of the stadium.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The collaboration kicks off immediately. Generali are now the Official Insurance Partner of the two clubs. The stadium naming rights contract will last for 25 years and includes the payment of €50m until the stadium opens in 2031.

This includes €10m annually for each of the next five seasons, including the current one, to be split equally between Inter and Milan. Once the stadium is inaugurated, the sum will increase to €30m annually (€15m each) for the following 20 years. The total commitment amounts to €650m.

Generali wanted to associate its name with the stadium from the very beginning of this long journey, in order to establish itself in the collective imagination: it will only be the Generali Stadium, with no temporary or secondary names to muddy the waters.

Branding a stadium from day one – or rather, even before it opens – is particularly strategic in terms of marketing. For this reason, Generali have agreed to pay €10m a year for something that, for five years, will be on models and then on construction sites, but not yet in the experience of fans.

Moreover, the naming rights market still has ample room for development in Europe. A Kroll study of the 36 major European clubs highlighted this already in 2024: only 12 had sold the naming rights to their stadiums, for a total estimated potential value of €303m, equivalent to an average of €8.4m per season.

Italy and England were the leagues least inclined to this form of commercialisation, also held back by the historical connection between the stadiums and their names. In this scenario, the new Inter and Milan stadium represents a different opportunity: a stadium to be built around a commercial identity,.

It’s not just a sports sponsorship: it’s a key support for a project crucial to the next stage of development of the city, the beating heart of the country’s economy and finances. This explains the significant investment.

It was the first time Inter and Milan worked together on the same side of the table, to secure a commercial deal. Under the watchful eye of Oaktree and RedBird Capital, the two clubs’ Chief Revenue Officers – Giorgio Ricci and Maikel Oettle – took the lead in finalising the sale of the naming rights.

These will be transferred through Stadio San Siro, a company controlled by NSM Holding, equally owned by Milan and Inter. The Rossoneri and Nerazzurri had initially selected a small group of potential partners, focusing on major Italian companies.

This type of right is particularly attractive to companies with a strong interest in the local area. After an initial round of meetings, Generali was the company that immediately showed the most enthusiasm.

Inter and AC Milan thus decided to further discuss the deal with the insurance giant, finalising the contract within a few months, the largest ever signed by Generali in the sports sector. In addition to the price offered, a key factor was the partner’s status.

Who better than the insurance giant – with €4.3bn in net profit and €900bn in assets under management – can offer guarantees of solidity and solvency, especially in a long-term commitment?

The €650m is written in stone and represents a significant portion of the financial coverage for the entire real estate transaction. Moreover, thanks to the €50m ‘bridge’ payment, the clubs have the opportunity to cash in immediately.

A comparison with other major international stadiums further illustrates the scale of the operation. Once fully operational, with €30m annually, the Generali Stadium will rank at the top of the global naming rights market, becoming one of the most lucrative deals ever signed for a stadium.

In Europe, the benchmark is Spotify’s Camp Nou, which guarantees Barcelona €20m per season, while Bayern Munich earn around €13m annually from Allianz for the Munich Arena. London’s Emirates Stadium is currently worth an estimated €10-15m to Arsenal.

Atletico Madrid’s Riyadh Air Metropolitano is at a similar level to that of the Gunners. In Germany, Borussia Dortmund get around €11m for Signal Iduna Park. Juventus, with the Allianz Stadium, earns around €8-10m.

To find higher figures, one must look primarily to the United States, where the stadium sponsorship market is of a different size and characteristics. The Crypto.com Arena in Los Angeles is worth approximately €31m annually, but the $700m twenty-year contract also includes other commercial activities of the AEG group and partnerships with the Lakers and Kings.

The same is true for SoFi Stadium in Los Angeles, whose twenty-year, $625m agreement equates to approximately €28m annually, but includes additional commercial components. Allegiant Stadium in Las Vegas, on the other hand, has signed a thirty-year agreement worth approximately $750m, equivalent to approximately €22m per season.

These transactions should be interpreted with caution, as the consideration does not always refer solely to the naming of the stadium. The Milan figure, however, is significant precisely because of the size of the naming rights component.

There is also another aspect to consider. The €30m guaranteed by Generali could represent only the first step in a much broader commercial strategy. An ecosystem of partnerships will develop around the new stadium, from technology services to beverage supplies, and other product categories that can be accommodated in the facility.

The goal is to raise an additional €20-25 millionm through agreements with secondary sponsors, bringing the overall value of the stadium’s commercial partnerships to even higher levels. Meanwhile, the market is also starting to move in Italy, albeit on a much smaller scale.

Atalanta earn approximately €2.5m annually from its partnership with New Balance for the naming of the New Balance Arena, while Udinese earns approximately €1m Bluenergy for the Bluenergy Stadium.

In short, the Milan deal risks rewriting the industry benchmarks, pushing up the valuations of other European venues and confirming how the name of a stadium, when associated with an international project, is a valuable asset.could become a top-level commercial asset.
